Discussion Name:
Will Sex Robots Advance Sexual Liberation?

1. Thesis:
Sex robots will advance sexual liberation.

  1.1. Pro:
  Sex robots could offer companionship to people who are lonely.

    1.1.1. Pro:
    Sex robots could help older people, who are often desexualised [1], have a fulfilling sexual life.

      1.1.1.1. Con:
      It is problematic to rely on sex robots to combat the loneliness that many older people experience, as sex robot use may exacerbate isolation.

        1.1.1.1.1. Con:
        Loss of sexual ability can erode someone's sense of identity (p. 29 [2]); sex robots remediate this for older people by creating benefits that outweigh possible loneliness.

        1.1.1.1.2. Pro:
        It is vital for sex robots to complement human interactions [3], not substitute for them, as technology is a long way away from being able to provide companion robots that can replace human interaction.

      1.1.1.2. Pro:
      Sex robots can be fashioned to look like deceased spouses [4], which can help with the grieving process; this could be very helpful for older widowers, who are less likely to find new partners.

        1.1.1.2.1. Con:
        For older people, sex robots may feel like a degrading replacement for their loss and worsen [5] their feelings of uncertainty and displacement. As such, it may be better to strengthen [6] existing bonds rather than replace them.

        1.1.1.2.2. Pro:
        Relationships help people age well [7]; as such, sex robots for those who have been widowed could offer a number of health benefits.

      1.1.1.3. Pro:
      The increase in life expectancy, combined with increasing divorce rates and declining marriage rates, means that there are many older individuals living alone with unfulfilled sexual needs [8].

        1.1.1.3.1. Pro:
        Older adults [9] can have medical conditions that decrease their libido and arousal. This may make it difficult for those still particularly interested in sex to find a partner, especially when compounded with issues like stigma, isolation and/or loss.

        1.1.1.3.2. Con:
        A study on the sexual activity and satisfaction of very old adults found that 67% of older people were happy with their current level of sexual activity (p. 9 [10]).

      1.1.1.4. Pro:
      Robotics are already becoming more common [11] in older people's lives; this may improve the prospects for successful uptake amongst aging populations.

        1.1.1.4.1. Con:
        Older people often eschew [12] technology as it does not align with their values, making the time investment of learning unrewarding; this may also apply to sex robots, reducing their capacity to help aging populations.

        1.1.1.4.2. Pro:
        Paro [13], a therapeutic robot, assists older people by imitating a pet, with proven positive results. Similar results could be achieved with sex robots.

      1.1.1.5. Con:
      Traditional views on sexuality can make it more difficult for older people to condone extramarital or casual sex (pp. 60-61 [14]); though this may change over time, it currently inhibits the liberatory potential of sex robots in this population.

        1.1.1.5.1. Pro:
        Cultural norms about sex have a greater effect on the sexual behavior of older people than their physical health (pp. 22, 27 [15]).

        1.1.1.5.2. Con:
        By the time sex robots are readily available, the vast majority [16] of older people may use cutting-edge technology which vastly improves their lives, making it easier to use sex robots in a positive manner.

    1.1.2. Pro:
    As LGBTQ+ people are often lonely [17], sex robots could be an invaluable tool for self-discovery and building confidence while they are coming to terms with their sexuality or struggling to meet partners.

      1.1.2.1. Con:
      To reduce loneliness in LGBTQ+ communities, it could be more productive to focus on efforts to reduce homophobia than to support sex robots, as the latter solution could be weaponised by conservatives to encourage abstinence politics that ultimately cause harm (p. 13 [18]).

        1.1.2.1.1. Pro:
        Since even those who support sex education [19] are often uncomfortable with normalising same-sex intercourse, sex robots could be used to derail growing social acceptance and foment homophobia.

        1.1.2.1.2. Pro:
        Sex is frequently used as a form of social control [20], so sex robots are likely to become an extension of this.

        1.1.2.1.3. Con:
        The conservative culture of silence around masturbation makes it unlikely that abstinence advocates will support sex robots (p. 10 [21]), even if it furthers their homophobic agenda.

      1.1.2.2. Con:
      It may not be possible to build confidence without the encouragement and support [22] of other humans. Internalised homophobia is likely to persist despite the use of sex robots.

      1.1.2.3. Con:
      The loneliness experienced by LGBTQ+ people is often connected to a lack of community [23], which sex robots cannot replace.

        1.1.2.3.1. Pro:
        Older LGBTQ+ people may struggle to find people like them, given the changing nature of the community [24]; sex robots are a poor fit for such needs.

        1.1.2.3.2. Pro:
        The stigma associated with sex robots may further compound the loneliness of LGBTQ+ people who use them and feel that they cannot connect meaningfully [25] with other people.

        1.1.2.3.3. Con:
        Gay men often use harmful coping mechanisms [26] to deal with loneliness. Sex robots may work as a harm-reducing [27] stopgap while other policies are implemented to help these men.

      1.1.2.4. Pro:
      LGBTQ+ people are more likely [28] to engage in risky sexual behaviours than their peers, leading to poor health outcomes [29]. Sex robots could be a useful alternative outlet.

        1.1.2.4.1. Pro:
        One consequence of risky sexual behavior faced by LGBTQ+ people is the possibility of contracting HIV or other STIs [30]. This would not be a concern with sex robots.

        1.1.2.4.2. Con:
        Research supports the position that risk-taking behaviours in transgender and gender non-conforming youth are a result of victimisation, so sex robots are unlikely to make a difference in this capacity (p. 158 [31]).

      1.1.2.5. Pro:
      For people with no alternative avenues for sex, sex robots [32] fill a vital gap. This could be especially impactful for LGBTQ+ people, who often struggle [33] to meet others.

        1.1.2.5.1. Pro:
        Studies have identified that transgender women may have a particularly difficult time finding intimate partners (p. 14 [34]).

        1.1.2.5.2. Con:
        There are a number of popular online dating apps that cater to LGBTQ+ people [35]. These are more likely to sate people's desire for emotional connection and sex than sex robots.

      1.1.2.6. Pro:
      An incidental development from Abyss Creations, primarily a sex robot manufacturer, is "partial-body dolls and wearable prosthetics" which, according to their CEO, are very popular amongst transgender customers [36].

        1.1.2.6.1. Pro:
        By allowing people to experiment with different types of sex organs, sex robots may help undermine bioessentialist [37] notions of gender.

    1.1.3. Pro:
    People who have medical or psychological conditions that make intimate relationships difficult can overcome their issues with sex robots.

      1.1.3.1. Con:
      While sex robots might help alleviate the sexual tensions associated with certain medical or psychological conditions (p. 29 [38], p. 4 [39]), they are unlikely to address the root causes, so their potential for liberation is limited.

        1.1.3.1.1. Pro:
        A sex robot cannot repair traumatic physical injury or offer real psychological support.

        1.1.3.1.2. Con:
        Promoting a healthy sex life for patients contributes to their overall well-being, in addition to improving immunity and reducing pain [40]. Generally improving someone's health can give them the energy to find other forms of sexual liberation or address the root causes of their sexual tensions.

        1.1.3.1.3. Con:
        RealDolls have claimed that sex robots have saved lives [41] by providing comfort and companionship.

          1.1.3.1.3.1. Con:
          Anecdotal claims by a sex robot manufacturer are not sufficient evidence that sex robots have lifesaving potential.

      1.1.3.2. Pro:
      Sex robots could be massively helpful to disabled users (p. 10 [42]), who are often sexually stigmatised (p. 160 [43]).

        1.1.3.2.1. Pro:
        Technology-based sex therapy can be helpful for those with traumatic spinal or genital injuries (p. 66 [44]). Sex robots may broaden the remit of this venture.

          1.1.3.2.1.1. Pro:
          Spinal injuries can make it difficult or impossible to assume many common sexual positions [45]. Sex robots can accomodate user positions in ways that humans cannot, greatly facilitating sexual activity.

        1.1.3.2.2. Con:
        Sex robots are currently marketed [46] towards non-disabled individuals, which may make them seem unsuitable for disabled people.

          1.1.3.2.2.1. Pro:
          Given the frequency [47] with which disabled people are ignored by non-disabled society, the presumption that sex robots are not suitable for them is likely to be rife.

          1.1.3.2.2.2. Con:
          Increasing awareness [48] of the ways that sex robots can help disabled people may nudge marketing departments to consider them as a core market and strive to make them seem more accessible.

        1.1.3.2.3. Pro:
        Disabled people often have reduced accessibility [49] to dating spaces; sex robots could help meet their sexual desires in an ableist world.

          1.1.3.2.3.1. Pro:
          Disabled people often face upsetting [50] or stigmatising [51] experiences when dating; sex robots could be a useful alternative for those that don't wish to risk such treatment.

          1.1.3.2.3.2. Con:
          If normalised, sex robots might become the sole romantic avenue for disabled people, further isolating them (p. 2 [52]). It is better to slowly but surely destigmatise disabled people than risk this harm.

      1.1.3.3. Pro:
      Sex robots could be used by people who have been traumatised or struggle with sexual relationships as part of their healing process [53].

        1.1.3.3.1. Pro:
        People who are healing from abuse or manipulation [54] still have sexual needs, although they may not be emotionally prepared to have human relationships.

          1.1.3.3.1.1. Con:
          Sex robots may cause harm if they displace human relationships and allow their users to reinforce negative thought patterns [55], rather than challenging them on them as, say, a friend might do.

          1.1.3.3.1.2. Pro:
          Hypersexuality [56] can arise due to trauma; it may be better for people to manifest this behaviour with sex robots than other people, as they are likely to be emotionally vulnerable.

        1.1.3.3.2. Con:
        Because sex with robots would be easier and more straightforward, human sexual relationships could become overwhelming; some patients recovering from trauma may be unable to make such a transition.

          1.1.3.3.2.1. Con:
          Some medical treatments are lifelong and focus on managing symptoms [57] rather than achieving a cure. It would not be harmful for sex robots to fall into this category, so long as patients are aware of this possibility and give informed consent [58].

          1.1.3.3.2.2. Pro:
          People have to want to get better [59] in order for therapeutic interventions to work. If improvement means losing access to a valued sex robot, then patients may feel that it is better not to try.

    1.1.4. Con:
    While some people may be satisfied by relationships with robots, many would be unfulfilled [60] by non-human relationships.

      1.1.4.1. Pro:
      Sex robots may fall into the uncanny valley [61], which could deter users from spending much time with them. This would decrease the potential for sex robots to replace human relationships.

        1.1.4.1.1. Pro:
        Humans typically struggle with the uncanny valley [62] feeling when robots look like humans, but move robotically. This disjunct would have to be remediated for sex robots to become popular.

        1.1.4.1.2. Con:
        The uncanny valley phenomenon continues to undergo exploration [63]; more recent studies suggest [64] that it might not exist or could be overcome, such that it would not inhibit sex robot adoption.

        1.1.4.1.3. Con:
        Robot Accommodation Process Theory (RAPT) suggests that, while there may be a point of distaste towards robots, there is a sharp increase in the acceptability of a given robot once it becomes mass-produced (pp. 280-281 [65]).

      1.1.4.2. Con:
      An empathy module [66] could allow robots to cater to the emotional and physical needs of their users. This would therefore limit social isolation by ensuring that humans are fully emotionally catered for.

        1.1.4.2.1. Con:
        A sex robot could not be programmed [67] to love their human sexual partner; this core emotional need is vital for fulfillment for many people, and as such, makes sex robots a poor substitute for other humans.

        1.1.4.2.2. Pro:
        Robots are starting to be able to recognise [68] human emotions and respond accordingly; sex robots with this capability would allow for reciprocity and a more emotional connection.

        1.1.4.2.3. Pro:
        In human relationships, falling in love [69] tends to include shared characteristics, either that exist before or develop during the relationship. If robots can be programmed to adapt similarly, then it may be possible to reciprocally fall in love with one.

          1.1.4.2.3.1. Con:
          Love is a reciprocal [70] process that depends not only on displays of affection, but also on the perceived emotions and thoughts driving the behaviour. As robots are likely to lack the inner life necessary for reciprocity, many would feel unfulfilled in these relationships.

          1.1.4.2.3.2. Pro:
          As people cannot perceive another's thoughts and feelings to such an extent that we would be conscious of their absence, a robot's lack of an inner life is irrelevant [71]. Feelings of reciprocal love can therefore arise.

      1.1.4.3. Pro:
      Even if sex robots provide sexual pleasure and the illusion of love, they remain incapable of choice [72], unlike humans [73]. As such, relationships with them will inevitably be unfulfilling.

        1.1.4.3.1. Pro:
        Robots have no autonomy with which to make choices, so are incapable [74] of a core element of love. Relationships with sex robots, thus, are not truly loving but rather parasitic.

        1.1.4.3.2. Con:
        Even if sex robots are not perfect companions, they are substantially better than total isolation and could act as useful adjuncts to other interventions (p. 3 [75]).

          1.1.4.3.2.1. Pro:
          The Roxxxy [76] sex robot was designed to help lonely, bereaved, or socially outcast men practice and improve their social interactions.

          1.1.4.3.2.2. Con:
          Using sex robots as a stopgap to alleviate social isolation could distract [77] from systemic change; such shifts require a critical mass of political will that sex robot use could easily undermine.

          1.1.4.3.2.3. Pro:
          Sex robots could be a viable solution for men who cannot find partners, as seen in China [78], where men outnumber women, leading to a massive jump [79] in the sex doll industry.

    1.1.5. Con:
    Sex with robots could be addictive and lead to people isolating [80] themselves from other humans. This may further widespread social isolation and loneliness [81].

      1.1.5.1. Con:
      The technology currently used in sex robots makes them suitable for sexual use but offers little [82] in the way of companionship. The necessary technology for such a use is likely still a long way off.

        1.1.5.1.1. Con:
        Around 25% of millenials [83] would consider dating a robot, indicating that the technology could help reduce loneliness.

          1.1.5.1.1.1. Con:
          The type of robot that many people envisage when they consider dating a robot is arguably impossible [84].

            1.1.5.1.1.1.1. Pro:
            People may envisage subconscious elements of communication in dating robots that they are currently far away [85] from being able to interpret or replicate.

          1.1.5.1.1.2. Pro:
          It is estimated that, by 2030, people will engage with virtual sex 'as casually as they browse porn today [86],' and that, by 2035, the majority of people will own sex toys with virtual reality elements.

        1.1.5.1.2. Con:
        Owners of sex dolls are frequently more concerned about interaction and personal intimacy than sex (pp. 30-31 [87]). This undermines the idea that sex robots cannot fulfil such needs.

          1.1.5.1.2.1. Pro:
          The creator [88] of Harmony sex dolls has said that his customers really want conversation and personality, to make sex robots more life like.

          1.1.5.1.2.2. Con:
          Niche sex doll forums [89] allow users to discuss and co-ordinate explicit use of sex dolls, away from the judgement and stigmatisation of others.

          1.1.5.1.2.3. Con:
          Regardless of reported intentions, the purchase of a sex robot implies that sex is essential to the user, as there are cheaper alternatives [90] available to meet emotional needs.

          1.1.5.1.2.4. Pro:
          On the primary online forum for current and prospective owners of RealDolls [91], the popular posts contain little or no sexual content. These are indicative of the fantasies that buyers are keen to perpetuate.

        1.1.5.1.3. Pro:
        For many people, the feeling of intimacy with another person is an important element of sex. Sex robots are unlikely to be able to replicate this.

          1.1.5.1.3.1. Pro:
          Reciprocity of desire from a sexual partner is central to the confidence we need as humans. Continued sexual activity with a robot that is programmed to be intimate may eventually lead to self-doubt and feelings of humilation [92].

          1.1.5.1.3.2. Con:
          Sex robots could be made to replicate intimacy, as is the case with virtual girlfriends [93]. The simulated affection from a virtual partner is appreciated, even if the simulation has no explicit sexual contact.

          1.1.5.1.3.3. Con:
          Sometimes people want to experience [94] an orgasm without the emotional attachments that can rise from sex with other people. Sex robots are ideal for such desires.

          1.1.5.1.3.4. Pro:
          Humans are unlikely to find emotional satisfaction with sex robots since they lack many of the characteristics of lasting relationships [95].

        1.1.5.1.4. Pro:
        Human relationships will continue to offer people more than relationships with sex robots until AI advances, which is unlikely to occur in the foreseeable future.

          1.1.5.1.4.1. Pro:
          Despite what we see in fiction and popular media, AGI [96] and strong AI [97] remain out of reach; the opinion of experts [98] points to 2075 as a possible year of their arrival.

          1.1.5.1.4.2. Con:
          Sufficently strong AI for emotional simulation may be possible; Pepper [99] is billed as the world's first emotional robot and uses an advanced AI to achieve this.

      1.1.5.2. Pro:
      As humans can become extremely attached to their machines by anthropomorphising them [100], sex robots may render human relationships unnecessary.

        1.1.5.2.1. Pro:
        Excessive sexual preoccupation with an inanimate object can constitute a fetishistic disorder [101]. If people feel sex robots can substitute for humans, they may use them to replace human relationships and cause undue harm.

          1.1.5.2.1.1. Con:
          42% of Americans name [102] their cars and form emotional bonds with them, yet society has not collapsed. It therefore seems unlikely that sex robots will cause significant changes to society.

            1.1.5.2.1.1.1. Con:
            Object sexuality [103] is distinct from simple utility. People do not develop sexual relationships with their cars.

          1.1.5.2.1.2. Pro:
          A majority of people superimpose the concept of a soul, brain, emotional state, and social rapport onto robots, in spite of their relative infancy (pp. 359-360 [104]). This may be exacerbated as robots develop.

        1.1.5.2.2. Pro:
        People may experience feelings of intimacy [105] with technology as they struggle with life in the 21st century. Encouraging this by normalising sex robots could be harmful in the long run, as people do not commit to making long term changes that benefit more people.

        1.1.5.2.3. Con:
        Humans genuinely [106] empathise with robots [107]. If human-robot relationships are functional and make people happy, there is little need to eschew them.

      1.1.5.3. Pro:
      Activists argue that sex robots are not an appropriate solution [108] for people who struggle to make human connections. They believe such people need therapy and support, not dolls.

        1.1.5.3.1. Pro:
        Distracting lonely people with sex robots is not ethical or desirable, since our financial and material well-being [109] in many cases is dependent on building relationships with other humans.

        1.1.5.3.2. Pro:
        The shut-in phenomena seems to be accelerating in advanced societies [110], and sex robots could reduce one of the few incentives to seek human contact.

          1.1.5.3.2.1. Pro:
          The personality traits associated with anime and manga audiences reveal some of the factors that may predispose potential users towards a favourable view of sex robots (p. 8 [111]), increasing their likelihood of becoming shut-ins.

          1.1.5.3.2.2. Con:
          Shut-ins are perfectly capable of making the rational assessment of what a good life looks like for them. It is illegitimate to suppress these people by requiring they adhere to societal norms.

          1.1.5.3.2.3. Pro:
          Sex robots could spell disaster for countries facing dwindling birth rates, such as Japan. An increasing number of men, known as 'otaku [112],' are eschewing human interaction in favour of cyber-reality or dolls [113].

        1.1.5.3.3. Con:
        In the future, there may be sex robots that require users to romance them [114] before any relationship can develop. This may facilitate learning for those who struggle with human interactions.

          1.1.5.3.3.1. Con:
          Once developed, customers may prefer only those sex robots that do not need to be romanced prior to use.

      1.1.5.4. Con:
      Sex is a morally relevant right [115] for the individual and contributes to personal and social welfare. This should be made available through sex robots, even if it increases isolation.

        1.1.5.4.1. Con:
        For those who are already lonely, increasing their sense of isolation by introducing them to sex robots could have negative effects on their health [116].

        1.1.5.4.2. Pro:
        People are capable [117] of determining the cost and benefits of any actions; if social interaction is worth less to someone than a sex robot, then they are free to make that choice.

  1.2. Con:
  Sex robots may encourage people to explore or develop harmful sexual preferences.

    1.2.1. Pro:
    Acting out and enjoying a taboo fantasy with a humanlike robot may encourage people to continue such behaviour with other humans.

      1.2.1.1. Pro:
      Having the ability to, for example, rape a realistic sex doll, amounts to positive reinforcement [118] of such behaviour (p. 64 [119]), potentially [120] making it more likely to occur in the future.

        1.2.1.1.1. Con:
        Rather than encouraging such behavior, the cathartic effects of acting out taboo fantasies may reduce the prevalence of rape and sexual assault (p. 10 [121]).

          1.2.1.1.1.1. Pro:
          Robots are not equivalent [122] to humans. One cannot say with certainty that behavior towards one would carry over to the other.

          1.2.1.1.1.2. Con:
          Both rape and victim blaming have frequently been characterised as power-motivated behaviors (pp. 1, 3 [123], p. 98 [124]); simulating a rape with a robot does not offer an opportunity for feeling or demonstrating power, and thus may be ineffective at reducing the prevalence of such crimes.

          1.2.1.1.1.3. Pro:
          Though there are multiple possible motivations for rape (p. 8 [125]), it remains a distinctively sexual act (p. 8 [126]). It is possible that in some cases, simulating the sexual element of rape might be sufficient to prevent actual rape.

        1.2.1.1.2. Pro:
        Despite being able to give cues consistent with the withdrawal of consent, sex robots may be unable to resist users. As such, users could become accustomed to ignoring revocations of consent.

        1.2.1.1.3. Con:
        According to the creators of a robot that resists sexual advances [127], the frigid settings are not meant to facilitate rape simulations, but rather to contrast with the model's eager personality setting.

      1.2.1.2. Pro:
      Those who seek to harm sex robots may have a socially problematic lack of moral sensitivity which could be encouraged through use of these devices (p. 22 [128]).

        1.2.1.2.1. Con:
        The human tendency to anthropomorphise robots may indicate that those with no empathy for humanoid robots may have maladaptive personality traits (p. 2 [129], p. 4 [130]). Given this, it is better these people harm robots than others humans.

        1.2.1.2.2. Pro:
        Repeated exposure to violence has been found to disrupt empathy (p. 12 [131]), and is a strong predictor of increased violent behavior. Thus, if people can abuse sex robots without social sanction, they may be more likely to harm other people.

      1.2.1.3. Con:
      Violent acts or thoughts towards non-humans do not correlate with violent acts against humans. Rather, the incidence of violence around the world is decreasing [132], despite the popularity of virtual violence (p. 5 [133]).

        1.2.1.3.1. Con:
        Some studies have shown that violent video games can increase aggressive thoughts and behaviors [134].

        1.2.1.3.2. Con:
        Studies have confirmed that violent ideation is a risk factor for future acts of violence against humans (p. 4 [135]).

          1.2.1.3.2.1. Con:
          The studies in question examined the relationship between ideation about and actual occurence of violence against humans. This is of limited application with regard to sex robots.

        1.2.1.3.3. Pro:
        Attempting to draw an association between acts against robots and acts against humans relies on anthropomorphism and analogies between robots and nonhuman animals (p. 1 [136]); this is misleading as it misunderstands the nature of a robot.

    1.2.2. Pro:
    Sex robots cannot give consent, making sex with them immoral. This is particularly true as they more closely approximate [137] human sentience.

      1.2.2.1. Con:
      Sex robots are not self-aware beings but animatronic objects. Whether or not people think robots can give consent is irrelevant, as they do not possess such faculties.

        1.2.2.1.1. Pro:
        Consent is not necessary for objects to fulfil their purpose; cars are not expected to consent to being driven.

        1.2.2.1.2. Pro:
        Expecting a robot to provide actual consent as opposed to the appearance of consent is anthropomorphism [138] and misunderstands the nature of AI.

          1.2.2.1.2.1. Pro:
          AI systems do not have autonomy [139] in the human sense, and as such are incapable of consent.

          1.2.2.1.2.2. Con:
          AI systems are developing in such a way that robots capable of human consent are likely in the near future (p. 317 [140]).

        1.2.2.1.3. Con:
        Although consent is not relevant to the sex robot, who cannot be a victim, it is relevant to the user, who, in the absence of consent, is symbolically imitating an act of rape on a humanoid being.

          1.2.2.1.3.1. Pro:
          In performing imitative symbolic behaviours, individuals normalise habits and practices that can have a direct and broad impact on society. This is made worse by the fact that people often ascribe [141] human-like emotions to robots.

      1.2.2.2. Pro:
      The legal world is considering the application of legal personhood [142] to robots with sufficient decision-making capacity. If granted the status of electronic persons with rights and obligations (p. 306 [143]), it is unethical to have sex with these robots without their consent.

        1.2.2.2.1. Con:
        Robots do not currently have sufficient autonomy to consent, nor is it necessary for them to be able to do so.

          1.2.2.2.1.1. Pro:
          Consent is considered a 'morally transformative act' (pp. 171-172 [144]), which—on the assumption that robots are not capable of being moral agents—renders sex robots incapable of giving consent.

          1.2.2.2.1.2. Con:
          Robots are already capable of being aware of their own defects [145]; when machines can overcome these defects, they have reached a point of de facto humanity.

        1.2.2.2.2. Con:
        Consent is a complicated act [146] with moral weight that sex robots need not be capable of taking on.

        1.2.2.2.3. Pro:
        As legal personhood is an artificial category [147], there is no reason why it cannot be applied to robots and AI.

      1.2.2.3. Pro:
      Desiring a partner without autonomy is problematic, as consent [148] is a key aspect of moral sexual behaviour. As sex robots become more difficult to distinguish from people, the norms of consent users establish when having sex with the former may be applied to the latter.

        1.2.2.3.1. Con:
        Desiring sex is not the same as desiring a nonautonomous partner. Sex robots can sate the former without encouraging the latter.

          1.2.2.3.1.1. Pro:
          It is not clear that interacting with a sex robot reinforces a notion of sex without consideration of the needs of a partner any more than vibrators or other sex toys do.

          1.2.2.3.1.2. Con:
          People may struggle [149] to distinguish robots from humans, as we have not been subject to evolutionary pressures that encourage such distinctions.

        1.2.2.3.2. Con:
        Sex robots could be designed to provide, withhold [150], or revoke consent [151], and could both require and be responsive to human consent.

          1.2.2.3.2.1. Pro:
          Sex robots that seek positive, enthusiastic consent cues could be useful [152] for those who have been traumatised by rape or sexual abuse, as it would reinforce normative, consensual relations.

          1.2.2.3.2.2. Con:
          It is unlikely that a feature to consent would be added to a sex robot design, as they are controlled by human users. Control over the use of sex robots is therefore presumed.

          1.2.2.3.2.3. Pro:
          Sex robots that can consent could be used as tools [153] to teach the importance of consent in sexual relationships, disrupting misogynistic beliefs about women and consent.

        1.2.2.3.3. Pro:
        Rape culture is perpetuated by problematic views about sex and consent. If sex robots are touted to offer a realistic sexual experience, it is problematic that they can be programmed to ignore consent cues.

          1.2.2.3.3.1. Con:
          Sex toy use is given meaning [154] by the user and is not inherent to the object itself.

          1.2.2.3.3.2. Pro:
          Problematic views of consent and sex may be carried through [155] to the relationships that people have with human partners, as a result of their interactions with sex robots.

    1.2.3. Con:
    The artificiality of sex robots allows immoral acts to be moral through the creation of moral distance (p. 24 [156]). This means that they could be used as a therapy tool for sex offenders.

      1.2.3.1. Pro:
      Virtual reality is already being used to help treat phobias, PTSD, and schizophrenia, so there is substantial precedent [157] for using technology in the treatment of other complex problems.

        1.2.3.1.1. Con:
        Attempting to conduct studies to assess the use of sex robots as a therapy for those with psychological disorders would be politically controversial [158] due to safety concerns, so such a use is improbable.

        1.2.3.1.2. Pro:
        Sex robots could have a cognitive behavioural therapy (CBT) [159] chat functionality to guide their users through therapy alongside human-led therapeutic interventions.

      1.2.3.2. Con:
      Paedophiles could be 'prescribed' child sex dolls to rehabilitate them; this may contribute to their psychological disorder by normalising negative behaviours (p. 402 [160]).

        1.2.3.2.1. Pro:
        If child sex robots are in legal circulation, there is the frightening possibility [161] of creating paedophiles.

        1.2.3.2.2. Con:
        One of the leading world suppliers of child sex dolls, Trottla, is run by Shin Takagi [162], who credits the dolls with preventing him from becoming a sex abuser.

          1.2.3.2.2.1. Pro:
          The Specialist Treatment Organization for the Prevention of Sexual Offending (StopSO)—a charity which prevents sexual offenses through therapy—believes that child sex robots could have a therapeutic use [163].

          1.2.3.2.2.2. Con:
          There is no evidence supporting the assertion that the use of sex dolls or robots will decrease sexual violence towards children or adults (p. 8 [164]).

        1.2.3.2.3. Pro:
        Police [165] have credited a surge in child sex doll purchases with helping uncover previously unknown paedophiles.

        1.2.3.2.4. Con:
        The use of child sex robots would be a victimless crime, much like computer-generated child pornography. If there is no harm caused by their production and usage, then there is no valid reason to prohibit child sex robots.

          1.2.3.2.4.1. Pro:
          Simulating [166] child pornography is legal in the US; there is no reason that child sex dolls would be prohibited under this mentality.

          1.2.3.2.4.2. Pro:
          The US Congressional child sex robot ban was not accompanied by substantial research on the subject, and may represent a prudish aversion to sexual imagination and expression [167].

          1.2.3.2.4.3. Con:
          Child pornography is often used by abusers to normalise behaviour and groom victims [168]; the same may well apply to child sex robots.

      1.2.3.3. Con:
      Legally, criminalising acts that constitute wrongful conduct is often necessary, even if there is no extrinsically harmful effect on others.

        1.2.3.3.1. Pro:
        Wrongful behaviour that can harm the moral character of people performing an act, even if they consent, can be criminalised (pp. 11-12 [169]).

        1.2.3.3.2. Con:
        The financial costs of attempting to regulate [170] the criminalisation of violence against sex robots may not be worth the moral benefit.

          1.2.3.3.2.1. Pro:
          As sex robots are likely to be typically used in private, monitoring [171] and reporting violent episodes may constitute a breach of privacy.

    1.2.4. Con:
    Having sex with an object—rather than a person with agency—may train people to give primacy to their own desires and ignore those of other people.

      1.2.4.1. Con:
      People typically give primacy to their own desires. This is not something that is trained into them by a robot. Being selfish is a precursor that leads to sex robot use, rather than a result.

        1.2.4.1.1. Pro:
        A degree of sexual selfishness [172] is normal and healthy. As such, sex robots may help users strike a balance between seeking and providing pleasure in sexual relationships with other humans.

        1.2.4.1.2. Con:
        Many people prefer sexual partners that are less selfish; learning to both give and receive pleasure [173] is essential to the cultivation of a healthy sexual relationship. Thus, sex robot use that undermines selflessness could reduce the pleasure of human-to-human sex.

      1.2.4.2. Pro:
      Sex with robots may promote digisexuality [174]: a state where one's sexual needs are exclusively filled by technological artifacts. Eschewing human interaction may be dangerous.

        1.2.4.2.1. Con:
        With respect to human sexuality, digisexuality could be considered a form of either abstinence, masturbation, or BDSM. None of these are inherently dangerous and so sex robot use should not be treated as such.

        1.2.4.2.2. Pro:
        Porn offers an experience distinct from sex yet can cause harmful gendered expressions of sexuality [175]. If sex robots exacerbate this, it may put people's partners at a disadvantage.

          1.2.4.2.2.1. Pro:
          Encouraging sex robot users to ignore the sexual desires of human partners is particularly problematic given the frequent erasure [176] of women's pleasure in heterosexual sex [177].

        1.2.4.2.3. Pro:
        Experts [178] predict that psychotherapists are likely to encounter a slew of individuals suffering from digisexuality; the fact that this will require therapeutic intervention indicates a pessimistic outlook that means sex robots are unlikely to advance sexual liberation.

        1.2.4.2.4. Con:
        An increase in digisexuality is not necessarily problematic. If the person is happy and nobody is harmed, then a person falling in love with a robot should be irrelevant to anybody else.

          1.2.4.2.4.1. Pro:
          The existence of non-reproductive, but nevertheless loving and reciprocal, relationships between humans and robots may help reduce overpopulation [179].

          1.2.4.2.4.2. Pro:
          As harmful and dysfunctional [180] interpersonal relationships are common [181], increasing the number of contented self-contained individuals in relationships with robot companions could be a positive development for society.

          1.2.4.2.4.3. Con:
          Digisexuality is likely to have significant repercussions on a person's relationships [182] with their family, friends, and acquaintances. Thus, human-robot relationships may have a net negative impact.

        1.2.4.2.5. Con:
        While there are emerging studies on the psychology of sex doll owners (p. 1 [183]), to date, there has not been a correlation between ownership and mental illness. As such, while digisexuality might be sexually deviant, it cannot be classified as a disorder.

      1.2.4.3. Con:
      The purpose of using a sex robot is self-pleasure. For this reason, the absence of concern about another is a selling point, not a disadvantage, of a sex robot.

        1.2.4.3.1. Pro:
        Masturbation and, by extension, sex robot use should be considered forms of self-care [184]; experiencing pleasure without worrying about another person's concerns can be relaxing and reduce stress.

        1.2.4.3.2. Con:
        For many people, the act of pleasing a partner [185] contributes substantially to their own pleasure. Thus, sex robot users who become accustomed to focusing solely on their own pleasure may actually reduce it.

      1.2.4.4. Pro:
      Sex robots could provide the zenith of sexual experience; users who become accustomed to this may be trained to believe their needs are primary.

        1.2.4.4.1. Pro:
        While lifelike in many ways, it is unlikely that sex robots will have the same capacity as humans to communicate using subtle cues, such as body language. This may normalise ignoring such cues with human partners.

        1.2.4.4.2. Con:
        The simulacrum of sex that robots offer is unlikely to ever be as realistic as with humans; users of sex robots will likely be able to distinguish the difference and behave accordingly in both contexts.

          1.2.4.4.2.1. Pro:
          -> See 1.2.1.1.1.1.

          1.2.4.4.2.2. Con:
          The desired level of reality proffered by sex robots among consumers varies. What one user finds unrealistic, another may find entrancing.

        1.2.4.4.3. Pro:
        Users could grow dependent on their robot partners, as they provide companionship without requiring reciprocity; users may experience resentment or irritation at a human partner's needs.

    1.2.5. Con:
    By acting as a substitute for commodified sex acts like pornography or sex work, sex robots could reduce the potentially criminal harms often associated with them.

      1.2.5.1. Pro:
      Sex robots, like other technologies [186], could improve the safety of sex workers, many of whom suffer from PTSD [187]. Sex robots could be used in place of women when the likelihood of violence is at its highest.

        1.2.5.1.1. Pro:
        Harassment and violence directed at sex workers is common. A review estimated that 45%-75% of sex workers experience violence during their work (p. 1 [188]).

        1.2.5.1.2. Con:
        People who frequently hire sex workers may do so because they desire intimacy [189] and closeness in a way that sex robots are unable to replicate.

        1.2.5.1.3. Con:
        The decriminalisation of sex work would be more likely to improve the safety of sex workers, as it is associated with the creation of better health programs (p. 486 [190]).

      1.2.5.2. Con:
      New technology can support and contribute to the sex industry. Sex robots may continue [191] this trend and therefore maintain or increase the harms [192] associated with sex work.

        1.2.5.2.1. Con:
        Technologies like online pornography displaced traditional media, as platform technologies provided better access to existing services. Sex robots, however, are an automative technology that supplants these services, providing different experiences.

        1.2.5.2.2. Pro:
        Positive experiences with sex robots may encourage people to seek out sex workers in the hope of having similar experiences that include the additional benefits of human interaction.

        1.2.5.2.3. Con:
        To the extent that pornography appears to offset instances of rape, sex robots could have a similar effect, helping to reduce the prevalence of sexual violence (p. 1 [193]).

        1.2.5.2.4. Pro:
        Sex robots are unlikely to reduce the stigma associated with sex work and may serve to further normalise the dehumanisation [194] of sex workers.

      1.2.5.3. Con:
      Sex work can be liberating. Many women find sex work to be enjoyable and profitable [195], so their displacement from this industry should not be lauded.

        1.2.5.3.1. Con:
        Those who pursue sex work because it is enjoyable or because it is highly profitable are likely to be people least affected by the harms of sex work.

        1.2.5.3.2. Con:
        To the extent that sex robots are an automated technology that supplant existing services, they are unlikely to supplant the jobs of sex workers who work for pleasure or offer premium services.

        1.2.5.3.3. Pro:
        If sex robots assume roles previously filled by humans, some sex workers may face a loss of earnings and possible unemployment [196].

          1.2.5.3.3.1. Con:
          Sex workers could play a valuable role [197] in the sex robot industry by assisting in the development of robot behaviours and voice acting.

          1.2.5.3.3.2. Pro:
          Given that brothels that offer sex dolls already exist in Germany [198], it seems likely that other popular sex tourism destinations will adopt them rapidly (p. 365 [199]).

          1.2.5.3.3.3. Pro:
          The displacement of sex workers by sex robots could cause the sex industry to go further underground, which may facilitate an increase in exploitative practices (p. 1634 [200]).

          1.2.5.3.3.4. Con:
          According to one study conducted across nine countries, 89% of sex workers wanted to leave their profession (p. 56 [201]).

      1.2.5.4. Pro:
      People working within the porn industry are often exploited [202]. It may be positive for these workers to be displaced by sex robots.

        1.2.5.4.1. Pro:
        Retired porn star, Lisa Ann [203], has spoken of the great pressure to participate in extreme porn, which can lead to unsafe situations.

        1.2.5.4.2. Con:
        Female porn stars are as psychologically healthy [204] as other women, with higher self-esteem and a better quality of life.

  1.3. Pro:
  Sex with robots could be better than sex with humans, offering new liberatory pleasures.

    1.3.1. Pro:
    Sex with robots may be more physically pleasurable than the alternatives available to users.

      1.3.1.1. Pro:
      Given the gendered orgasm gap [205], sex robots could allow heterosexual women to have better and more liberating sexual experiences than sex with their partners currently allows.

        1.3.1.1.1. Con:
        Relative to their male counterparts, few women express an interest [206] in having sex with robots. Given this, liberating sexual experiences, if possible, are likely to be few and far between.

          1.3.1.1.1.1. Pro:
          Two-thirds of surveyed [207] women said they would not have sex with a robot, suggesting this may not be a plausible option for advancing their sexual liberation.

          1.3.1.1.1.2. Con:
          Many women use dildos and other sex toys [208]. As sex robots become more common, women may become more accepting of them as well.

            1.3.1.1.1.2.1. Con:
            Despite the high percentage [209] of women who use sex toys, the number of women who report experiencing shame at doing so remains high (p. 201 [210]). The low reported interest in sex robots may be related to social perception rather than actual interest.

          1.3.1.1.1.3. Con:
          In only a few decades, the sex toy industry rocketed [211] from niche to mainstream popularity with women, facilitated by visibility in popular culture; sex robots may follow the same trend.

          1.3.1.1.1.4. Pro:
          Women are often influenced [212] by context, environment, and social pressures when shaping their sexual desires. As long as sex robots remain niche, disgust may thwart women's interest in them.

        1.3.1.1.2. Con:
        The traditional female sexual strategy encourages women to highly value the formation of emotional connections through sex (p. 4 [213]); women may lack interest in sex with robots since it would not achieve this goal.

          1.3.1.1.2.1. Pro:
          Variability and discrepancy in emotional response is a key element in the development of human emotional bonds (p. 5 [214]). Sex robots are not capable of such variability, so humans are unlikely to form such bonds with robots.

          1.3.1.1.2.2. Con:
          Women who denounce traditional gender roles can be role models [215] for others; as such, those who use sex robots can normalise the idea that women do not need to have an emotional connection to enjoy sex.

          1.3.1.1.2.3. Con:
          Women often have sex drives as strong as [216] men and are not necessarily motivated by a need for emotional intimacy. Therefore, women may still enjoy using sex robots.

        1.3.1.1.3. Pro:
        There are established links between female masturbation and increased sexual pleasure (p. 1 [217]); women who have sex with robots may experience the same positive benefits.

          1.3.1.1.3.1. Pro:
          Women are often shamed [218] for having active sex lives, which may quell their pleasure. As sex robots are likely to be private devices, they may help women to enjoy themselves without judgement.

            1.3.1.1.3.1.1. Pro:
            Women are not typically shamed for using sex toys; in some instances, they are encouraged [219]. A similar perspective may apply to sex robots.

            1.3.1.1.3.1.2. Con:
            Double standards regarding the sex lives of men and women will persist despite sex robots. Just as women are judged for having a lot of sex, they may also be judged for using sex robots.

          1.3.1.1.3.2. Con:
          It could be difficult to construct a robot that is sexually fulfilling for all women, as they have variable sexual experiences [220] and preferences [221].

            1.3.1.1.3.2.1. Pro:
            There is no specific sexual technique [222] that is guaranteed to bring women to orgasm, which may inhibit the success of sex robots.

            1.3.1.1.3.2.2. Con:
            Sex robots for women can integrate elements of touch, breath, and smell [223], which contribute directly to women's arousal. Sex with these robots could thus be far better for women.

          1.3.1.1.3.3. Pro:
          Masturbation is not only [224] about sexual pleasure; it has positive effects on mood, self-esteem, bodily aches, and stress. A sex robot could help women in all these areas.

          1.3.1.1.3.4. Con:
          Even though masculinised sex robots have entered the market, the complications [225] in designing lightweight, user-friendly robots may may make them less popular than their feminine counterparts.

        1.3.1.1.4. Pro:
        Women may not find relationships with men sexually fulfilling since there is often a focus on penetrative sex, and only 25% of women [226] can orgasm through solely [227] vaginal intercourse. Given this, sex robots may be preferable.

      1.3.1.2. Pro:
      Robots can be configured with characteristics that are more pleasurable than those of human anatomy, or have features added and removed at will.

        1.3.1.2.1. Con:
        Uncoupling sex robots from human physiology could negatively impact human sexual relationships by priming people to expect features that do not exist in humans.

        1.3.1.2.2. Pro:
        Sex robots are not limited [228] by human physiology—they can be 9 feet tall or have working prehensile tails. This could open up new avenues [229] of sexual exploration and spur liberation.

      1.3.1.3. Pro:
      People can use sex robots to explore sexual interests that their partners are uninterested or unwilling to engage in. Increasing people's sexual autonomy in this way contributes directly to their liberation.

        1.3.1.3.1. Con:
        Sex robots may expose couples to difficult questions, such as whether it is considered cheating to have sex with a sex robot. This may be harmful on net.

          1.3.1.3.1.1. Con:
          It is positive for people in relationships to have open and honest conversations about their boundaries [230]. Exposure to sex robots and the issues they present may promote better communication between partners.

          1.3.1.3.1.2. Con:
          Sex with robots can only be considered cheating [231] if women using vibrators is also treated as such. As the latter is generally considered acceptable, the former should not disrupt relationships.

          1.3.1.3.1.3. Pro:
          42% of respondents felt that sex with robots would constitute cheating on a partner, showing that this is likely to be a major concern for users (p. 232 [232]).

        1.3.1.3.2. Pro:
        Sex robots allow users to freely explore their sexuality without being constrained by the desires, hang-ups, or judgement of a partner. This promotes healthy sexuality and strengthens their sexual identity (p. 7 [233]).

          1.3.1.3.2.1. Pro:
          Recent research suggests that those who develop their sexuality in isolation [234] exhibit increased agency with regard to sexual orientation and less reliance on restrictive norms.

          1.3.1.3.2.2. Con:
          Many sexual norms, such as those embodied in comprehensive sex education programs, promote the safety [235] and health [236] of individuals. Sex robot users straying from these norms could, thus, be harmful.

        1.3.1.3.3. Pro:
        Sex robots could help people overcome anxieties [237] and discover their fetishes in a safe and consensual manner.

          1.3.1.3.3.1. Con:
          Since sex robots do not make judgements about users in the way that other humans will, the benefits of sexual exploration with robots may not carry over to human partners.

          1.3.1.3.3.2. Pro:
          Normalising BDSM—traditionally a niche interest—would be a positive development [238] for sexual liberation. Sex robots could facilitate this and accomplish a great deal of good (pp. 1-2 [239]).

            1.3.1.3.3.2.1. Pro:
            As a user's activity with sex robots remains entirely confidential, BDSM with sex robots would mitigate user concerns about the social stigma associated with disclosing BDSM interest (p. 58 [240]).

            1.3.1.3.3.2.2. Con:
            Many of the common reasons [241] why people choose to participate in BDSM—such as improving trust, intimacy, and communication between partners—are not available with sex robots.

            1.3.1.3.3.2.3. Con:
            BDSM encourages unhealthy behaviours and attitudes, so it should not be considered liberatory (p. 9 [242]).

              1.3.1.3.3.2.3.1. Con:
              Sex robots could be programmed to unfailingly respect the user's consent, removing concerns about abusive and unsafe behaviour during BDSM scenes (p. 18 [243]).

              1.3.1.3.3.2.3.2. Pro:
              The normalisation of BDSM has serious real-world consequences in cases of sexual violence [244], especially against women.

              1.3.1.3.3.2.3.3. Con:
              BDSM practices can result in physical harm [245] to others in a sexual context; using a sex robot may allow people to freely enjoy BDSM without physical repercussions.

      1.3.1.4. Con:
      In much the same way as pornography, there is a risk of people conditioning [246] themselves to only experience an orgasm or become aroused with a sex robot.

        1.3.1.4.1. Pro:
        Human sexual arousal can be conditioned to become responsive to elements that do not correspond with sexual interactions between humans (p. 14 [247]).

        1.3.1.4.2. Con:
        Some of the elements of internet pornography that contribute to conditioning someone's arousal and pleasure, such as surfing, are not present in sex robots (p. 390 [248]).

      1.3.1.5. Con:
      Sex robots merely replicate a physical sensation and cannot provide subtle physical and psychological elements of human sex. Without these, sex with robots may quickly become repetitive and uninteresting.

        1.3.1.5.1. Con:
        The assumption that human sex is objectively better than robot sex presupposes an ideal that defies the premise of sexual liberation, which is to enable people to explore their individual sexual preferences.

        1.3.1.5.2. Con:
        Some sex toys [249] incorporate sensors to assess the optimal type of stimulation to improve a user's experience. These could be used in sex robots.

        1.3.1.5.3. Pro:
        Sex robots are designed solely to help the user climax. This bypasses the usual characteristics of good sex [250]. As robots can only supply physical stimulation, sex with them is unlikely to rival sex with other people.

    1.3.2. Pro:
    Sex robots would allow people to have sex without concern for pregnancy or health risks. Such concerns may inhibit people, particularly where they cause acute anxiety [251].

      1.3.2.1. Con:
      There are many forms of sex that hold no risk of pregnancy.

        1.3.2.1.1. Pro:
        Any type of sex [252] that does not allow semen to enter the vagina [253] makes pregnancy impossible.

        1.3.2.1.2. Con:
        Penis-in-vagina penetrative sex is common among heterosexual people, so they risk pregnancy [254] even while using birth control.

      1.3.2.2. Con:
      An increase in sex with robots would not necessarily decrease the amount of sex between humans.

        1.3.2.2.1. Pro:
        Increased masturbation frequency does not decrease [255] the frequency of sex. Thus, sex robot use is unlikely to displace sexual activity.

        1.3.2.2.2. Con:
        Since sex robots are not limited by human endurance (p. 60 [256]), sex with robots may leave users too physically exhausted to pursue sex with humans as often.

        1.3.2.2.3. Con:
        Young people are already having less sex than in previous generations [257]. Anything that decreases their motivation for seeking human partners is likely to contribute further to this phenomenon.

        1.3.2.2.4. Pro:
        Having sex more frequently is associated with an increased sex drive [258]. Thus, sex with robots could plausibly increase the amount of sex between humans.

      1.3.2.3. Con:
      Condoms [259] are effective at reducing the risk of pregnancy and STIs and may remain more popular than sex robots.

        1.3.2.3.1. Con:
        Despite widespread availability of effective birth control methods, such as condoms [260] and birth control pills [261], many people do not take advantage of them.

        1.3.2.3.2. Con:
        No birth control method is 100% effective [262], unlike robots who are physically incapable of producing offspring.

        1.3.2.3.3. Pro:
        If robots are shared between multiple individuals, the risk of disease transmission could [263] still be present, especially if hygiene norms are not strictly adhered to.

          1.3.2.3.3.1. Pro:
          It may be hard to tell visually that a sex robot has been thoroughly cleaned [264], making it harder for those sharing them to protect themselves from the spread of STDs.

          1.3.2.3.3.2. Pro:
          Sex robot brothel owners may wish to cut the costs associated with cleaning their devices, as hot water [265] could damage their electrics, increasing the risk of clients contracting STIs.

          1.3.2.3.3.3. Con:
          Removable vaginas [266] decrease a user's risk of catching an STI through sex doll use.

      1.3.2.4. Pro:
      Young people are frequently taught that abstinence is the only method which ensures they will avoid pregnancy and STDs (p. 1 [267]), yet they still have sexual urges. Sex robots could be a safe mechanism for young people to explore their sexuality.

        1.3.2.4.1. Pro:
        Sex robots could supplement sex education by teaching sexual customs and practices to adolescents (pp. 354-355 [268]).

        1.3.2.4.2. Con:
        If young people spend their formative years interacting with robots instead of humans, they may not develop important social skills, such as an understanding of consent and respect for bodily autonomy (p. 2 [269]).

        1.3.2.4.3. Con:
        Young people are unlikely to access sex robots due to the cost and the societal shame [270] of divulging details of their sex lives to their parents.

        1.3.2.4.4. Pro:
        Parents who understand the need for a safe sexual outlet may purchase sex robots for their children once they reach an acceptable age just as they do with other sex toys [271].

      1.3.2.5. Pro:
      Sex is not only about procreation; it is a vital recreational activity which has tremendous social and personal impacts. It could be highly beneficial to facilitate this using sex robots.

        1.3.2.5.1. Pro:
        Sex often makes people happier [272]. If sex robots make it easier to fulfil this desire then they may bring about a net-positive change in society.

          1.3.2.5.1.1. Con:
          In order for sex robots to sate people's desire to have more sex, users would have to view sex with robots as an equivalent to sex with humans. This may not be the case.

            1.3.2.5.1.1.1. Pro:
            In the same way that sex toys [273] cannot replace human anatomy, it is unlikely that robots would be able to fully replace sex with humans.

          1.3.2.5.1.2. Pro:
          A survey found that 53.2% of women would like to have sex more frequently [274] than they currently do. Sex with robots could help address this.

          1.3.2.5.1.3. Pro:
          Sex robots could be used by people without partners to experiment without the hazards [275] that go along with one night stands or casual hook-ups.

            1.3.2.5.1.3.1. Con:
            Humans that are attracted to the challenge and excitement [276] associated with meeting new sexual partners are likely to be less interested in the high availability of sex robots.

            1.3.2.5.1.3.2. Pro:
            Women often regret [277] casual hookups and feel shame and depression in many scenarios. Such outcomes are less likely with sex robots.

            1.3.2.5.1.3.3. Pro:
            People who choose to have casual sex must avoid STDs [278], which would not be a concern with robots.

        1.3.2.5.2. Pro:
        Sex has a vast number of positive health benefits [279], from assisting sleep to improving blood pressure and reducing the instances of prostate cancer in men. Sex robots could therefore directly benefit the user's health.

        1.3.2.5.3. Con:
        Many of the benefits of sex are attributed to the psychological effect of intimacy with another person, rather than purely sexual mechanisms. Sex robots cannot compensate for the absence of another person.

          1.3.2.5.3.1. Con:
          Masturbation increases cortisol levels [280], which can lead to improvements in immune function. Since this occurs despite the lack of a human partner, sex robots may offer a similar benefit.

          1.3.2.5.3.2. Pro:
          Those who engaged in penile-vaginal sex had lower stress and better blood pressure, likely due to oxytocin [281], than those who engaged in masturbation or non-coital sex.

          1.3.2.5.3.3. Pro:
          Sex is more physiologically satisfying than masturbation, which may include sex with robots (p. 312 [282]). Prolactin, a vital hormone, increases 400% more [283] post-intercourse than post-masturbation.

    1.3.3. Con:
    Reciprocal desire is a key element in the development of romantic feelings, which can improve sex [284] (p. 1335 [285]). Sex robots are unlikely to be able to simulate this, as sex robots do not experience genuine emotions.

      1.3.3.1. Pro:
      Researchers have identified that rationality and order [286], which are inherent in the design of robots, are often opposed to female desire.

        1.3.3.1.1. Pro:
        Predictability of response is assumed in robot design (p. 6 [287]); this stands in stark contrast to the variability of human reactions and so may be off-putting for women who otherwise seek emotional relationships with sex robots.

        1.3.3.1.2. Con:
        Other research has demonstrated that robots that are programmed to behave unpredictably at times can increase user engagement and anthropomorphisation (p. 1 [288]).

      1.3.3.2. Con:
      If the robot can behave [289] as it has feelings and exhibits genuine emotions, this may be enough to satisfy people.

        1.3.3.2.1. Con:
        Even if robots are able to convincingly simulate reciprocal desire, human users may find it impossible to achieve due to the inherent power imbalance [290] in human-robot interactions.

        1.3.3.2.2. Pro:
        Sex robots are likely to guarantee expressions of reciprocity, as opposed to human interactions which can often be uncertain (p. 1334 [291]).

      1.3.3.3. Pro:
      According to some experts, “being desired is the orgasm [292]" for women. Sex robots are unlikely to inspire this feeling in their owners, so may offer limited pleasure.

        1.3.3.3.1. Con:
        While sexual desire begins in the mind and is often necessary for arousal, the experience of being desired on its own will generally not lead to orgasm [293] for most women.

        1.3.3.3.2. Pro:
        Though people can become attached [294] to objects, it is unlikely for them to form romantic attachments with them. This may inhibit women's sexual pleasure when using sex robots.

      1.3.3.4. Con:
      Humans become aroused [295] when touching robots in intimate locations, indicating that people feel desire for them. This may mean that sex robots are able to provide the requisite features necessary for developing romantic feelings.

        1.3.3.4.1. Con:
        Arousal is not necessarily a precursor for intimacy. Rather, for many people, sex is only moral when accompanied by emotional bonding (p. 35 [296]). As such, even if someone is aroused by robots, they may forgo sex with them if they don't believe that it can lead to romantic love.

        1.3.3.4.2. Pro:
        The human tendency to anthroporphise and be aroused by robots may mean that users can form emotional connections with them. Over a quarter of surveyed people believed that robots would experience psychological responses if used for sex (p. 254 [297]).

  1.4. Con:
  Sex robots promote misogyny, which actively curtails sexual liberation, especially for women.

    1.4.1. Pro:
    Violent and demeaning porn influences men's behaviour and attitudes towards women by dehumanising [298] them. Sex robots may also contribute to this phenomenon (p. 1666 [299]), making intimate relationships with women less likely.

      1.4.1.1. Con:
      Pornography often reduces women to objects without sexual agency (p. 536 [300]). Sex robots could help mitigate this, if used as a substitute.

        1.4.1.1.1. Con:
        Just as consuming porn negatively alters the expectations and preferences of the viewer [301], using sex robots could negatively change the expectations and desires of the users.

        1.4.1.1.2. Pro:
        There is a growing trend towards ethical porn [302], which aims to be non-exploitative and more realistic. This may mean that sex robots can only minimally change the industry.

        1.4.1.1.3. Con:
        As pornography is beneficial [303] to viewers, displacing consumption by normalising sex robots may slow sexual liberation.

        1.4.1.1.4. Pro:
        Some believe that pornography causes addiction [304] and negatively impacts one's sexual experiences. Sex robots could displace porn use to advance sexual liberation.

        1.4.1.1.5. Con:
        Sexual objectification is not exclusive to women as men experience similar effects, albeit in different ways [305].

      1.4.1.2. Pro:
      Men's frequency of exposure to pornography and lifestyle magazines that objectify women predict stronger attitudes supportive of violence against women (p. 955 [306]). Sex robots could function in a similar manner.

        1.4.1.2.1. Pro:
        Some studies indicate a correlative link between pornography use and increased tolerance for sexual aggression (p. 31 [307]). Sex robot use may induce similar effects.

        1.4.1.2.2. Con:
        Other studies suggest that there is a minimal link [308] between pornography and sexual aggression. As such, it cannot be claimed that sex robots will lead to harm by referring to the influence of pornography.

      1.4.1.3. Con:
      Since there is a lack of research about the effects of sex robot use on men's behaviour and attitudes towards women, there is insufficient evidence that usage will produce these effects.

        1.4.1.3.1. Pro:
        There are differences in the experience of viewing pornography versus using sex robots that may make it difficult to apply findings from other research areas (p. 4 [309]).

        1.4.1.3.2. Con:
        The risk that sex robots could cause more sexual violence or worsen societal misogyny is cause enough to believe they will not further sexual liberation.

        1.4.1.3.3. Con:
        Should people perceive, incorrectly or otherwise, that sex robots increase sexual violence or misogyny, they are also likely to believe that sex robots hinder society and set back sexual liberation.

        1.4.1.3.4. Pro:
        Many researchers in the field agree that existing studies [310] into the effects of pornography are low quality and heavily biased. If research cannot be conducted well into the widespread phenomenon of pornography, sex robots are unlikely to encounter fair study.

    1.4.2. Pro:
    As women hold less than 25% [311] of the jobs in technology and engineering, sex robots are likely to be created predominantly by men (thus, with their preferences in mind) which may limit their liberatory potential.

      1.4.2.1. Con:
      Many techologies designed by men are used by women. The gender of a sex robot designer does not restrict its eventual user base, nor limit the ability to conduct market research [312] that adheres to this group's needs.

        1.4.2.1.1. Pro:
        Robots designed for women or gay men are unlikely to promote misogyny as they are overwhelmingly likely to be masculinised; the method of posing and marketing these robots could be used as models for those marketed to heterosexual men.

        1.4.2.1.2. Con:
        The sex robot industry is likely to be affected by the male-dominated consumer market [313], as sex robots are designed to correspond to this group's desires.

      1.4.2.2. Pro:
      Due to the male gaze [314], male engineers in robotics may transfer their heteronomative and sexualised views of women onto objects. This may limit the scope and design of sex robots.

        1.4.2.2.1. Pro:
        In 2017, there were no women on the Realbotix [315] development team for the feminised AI used in Harmony sex dolls. Having a sex doll with a personality entirely designed by men may encourage regressive gender norms.

        1.4.2.2.2. Con:
        Sinthetics, one of the largest sex doll companies worldwide, has a number of women [316] working on their sex robots. This may counteract misogyny in the company's designs.

        1.4.2.2.3. Con:
        Sexism in artificial intelligence is being remediated [317] with time, as robots are increasingly built with algorithms that place primacy on sentiment analysis instead of gender.

        1.4.2.2.4. Pro:
        The creator of the Fleshlight submitted a patent for a sex doll, citing a desire to build a doll whose compliance stands in contrast to women's "cruel" autonomy (p. 27 [318]).

      1.4.2.3. Pro:
      Two feminised robot strippers made participants of a tech conference [319] feel uncomfortable. Such norms deter women from tech and thus may allow sex robots to propagate sexist norms unchallenged.

        1.4.2.3.1. Con:
        The presence of feminised robot strippers at CES is of lesser import for women in tech than the failure of the conference to include female speakers [320] for two consecutive years.

        1.4.2.3.2. Pro:
        Some attendees at CES 2018 believed [321] that robot strippers could be empowering for women—such misconceptions indicate that the industry has far to go in order to become truly egalitarian.

    1.4.3. Con:
    As women's social status continues to increase (p. 123 [322]), instances of misogyny are likely to become less common. Thus, misogyny is not a significant threat to modern society.

      1.4.3.1. Pro:
      Instances of misogyny are effects, not causes, of women's historically lower social status [323], which may be rooted in women's dependence on men for economic support.

      1.4.3.2. Pro:
      Research indicates that the origin of misogyny is rooted in the historic shift to agricultural production [324] and the associated rise of patriarchal society. Sex robots neither support nor undermine patriarchal structures; they cannot play a role in the perpetuation of misogyny.

      1.4.3.3. Con:
      Levels of abuse and violence towards women and girls have remained level [325] in recent years. Any increase in misogyny that correlates with increased violence against women and girls could be significantly harmful.

        1.4.3.3.1. Pro:
        An average of 3 women per day are murdered [326] by their intimate partner in the US, and nearly half [327] of all women murdered are killed by a romantic partner.

        1.4.3.3.2. Con:
        The assertion that sex robot use will lead to higher incidence of violence against women and girls is a slippery slope [328] argument.

        1.4.3.3.3. Pro:
        There is increasing support for considering sex- and gender-driven violence as a hate crime [329]; misogyny directly contributes to violence against women.

      1.4.3.4. Con:
      As society is far [330] from achieving gender equality, any increase in misogyny would be a horrendous step backwards.

        1.4.3.4.1. Pro:
        Across the globe, many harmful beliefs [331] perpetuate misogyny.

          1.4.3.4.1.1. Pro:
          In many places, women are expected not to deny [332] their male partners sex. Sex robots—who by their nature do not deny sex—are likely to only strengthen this expectation.

          1.4.3.4.1.2. Con:
          Like pornography [333], sex robots could be built to support progressive liberatory sexual ethics that undermine misogyny.

          1.4.3.4.1.3. Pro:
          Expectations that women be beautiful for men fuel gender inequality [334]; building feminised sex robots that support the view that women exist solely for men's pleasure could reinforce this.

        1.4.3.4.2. Con:
        There is a wide array of actors, from government organisations [335], NGOs [336], feminist communities [337], and more, working to challenge misogyny. It is unlikely that sex robots will be able to significantly undermine the cultural impact of these groups.

        1.4.3.4.3. Pro:
        Gender inequality causes huge economic and social problems [338] (p. 2 [339]), and endangers the safety [340] of women. These harms should not be dismissed, especially if exacerbated by sex robots.

    1.4.4. Con:
    Sex robots could bring about a major sexual revolution for women, as sexual enoucounters with robots are likely to be safer than those with other humans. Thus, sex robots could offer the benefits of casual sex with lower risks.

      1.4.4.1. Pro:
      Women are often vulnerable to sexual violence during one-night stands (pp. 1-2 [341]). Sex robots could be a useful outlet for sexual desires to avoid such danger.

        1.4.4.1.1. Pro:
        Increased participation [342] in casual sex is associated with increased proclivity toward sexual harassment; making the former less common may make women safer.

          1.4.4.1.1.1. Con:
          If participation in casual sex increases likelihood of sexually harassing others, then sexual harassment could increase if sex robots were seen as a form of casual sex.

          1.4.4.1.1.2. Pro:
          If women use sex robots as an outlet for casual sex, they may be less likely to seek out others and so drive down the rate of such behaviour. This reduction in casual sex could in turn improve people's mental health [343].

        1.4.4.1.2. Con:
        Sex toys may not displace sexual desire but rather increase [344] it by offering a diversity of pleasure. Thus, women may continue to seek casual sex that puts them in danger, even as they use sex robots.

        1.4.4.1.3. Pro:
        Sex robots may displace one-night stands, as masturbation can decrease a desire for partnered sex (p. 230 [345]). This may make women safer, even as they sate their sexual desires.

      1.4.4.2. Pro:
      People rejected after casual sex may become stalkers [346]. Women, who may be particularly at risk of such threatening treatment, could use sex robots to avoid this while getting to know suitors.

        1.4.4.2.1. Con:
        Stalking often occurs [347] after an intimate relationship has ended and despite a suitor being initially well-vetted. Given this, sex robots may have little effect on the frequency of such behaviours.

        1.4.4.2.2. Pro:
        The companionship of a sex robot may make women less vulnerable. If they have a backup option should dates go wrong, women may be more likely to leave dangerous situations instead of holding out for them to improve.

      1.4.4.3. Con:
      Women may be more likely to have casual sex with friends [348] than use sex robots if they wish to avoid the risks of sex with strangers, as sex robots are likely to be a long-term financial investment.

        1.4.4.3.1. Con:
        Human relationships, and in particular those requiring a level of trust, require a substantial time investment that would not be necessary with sex robots (p. 149 [349]).

          1.4.4.3.1.1. Pro:
          Human choice consistently reflects a preference for added convenience [350].

          1.4.4.3.1.2. Con:
          Since research has demonstrated that the size of the investment in romantic relationships is directly related to their perceived value (pp. 183-184 [351]), low-investment robot relationships are unlikely to replace high-investment human ones.

        1.4.4.3.2. Pro:
        Currently, simple sex robots cost between US$5,000 and US$15,000 [352], and this will likely increase as technology becomes better and more complex [353], making them significant investments (p. 251 [354]).

          1.4.4.3.2.1. Pro:
          The animatronic AI head [355] developed by Abyss creations costs $10,000 USD on its own.

          1.4.4.3.2.2. Pro:
          Further advances in AI are likely to be very expensive since the technology will have high complexity and performance [356] requirements.

          1.4.4.3.2.3. Con:
          Cutting-edge technology is often expensive; market forces consistently drive the price of technology down [357] over time.

      1.4.4.4. Con:
      Casual sex can boost [358] people's self-esteem; using sex robots by comparison may not. As such, displacing the former with the latter may inhibit sexual liberation.

        1.4.4.4.1. Con:
        Masturbation can boost people's self-esteem [359]; there is no reason why sex robot use would not provide the same benefit in this regard as casual sex.

        1.4.4.4.2. Pro:
        Sex robots remove the motivation for those with self-esteem issues [360] to seek help and could exacerbate existing emotional problems.

    1.4.5. Pro:
    Most sex robots are produced [361] with an idealised and stereotypical feminine form. This may contribute to women's body image issues and hinder sexual liberation.

      1.4.5.1. Pro:
      Many sex dolls currently use gendered racist stereotypes that fetishise certain ethnicities [362]. Sex robots are likely to use these stereotypes to further harm women of colour.

        1.4.5.1.1. Con:
        Ethnic stereotyping is perpetuated by dolls generally; many toys for girls make use of essentialised ethnic features [363] and stereotypical bodies and dress. Nonetheless, such dolls can be viewed as liberatory [364], and so sex robots may be able to achieve similar goals.

        1.4.5.1.2. Con:
        As it becomes more possible for users to specify the design of sex robots [365], it is likely that stereotypical models necessitated by the limitations of mass production will increasingly give way to more personalized robots designed around a single user's preferences.

        1.4.5.1.3. Pro:
        Racial fetishism [366] is rife in society; the budding sex robot industry has the potential and the responsibility to avoid such common pitfalls of the sex industry.

      1.4.5.2. Pro:
      As feminised sex robots are often designed to simulate a man's 'ideal woman' in both appearance and behaviour, patriarchal norms of desire [367] may be reinforced.

        1.4.5.2.1. Con:
